the 3 non-collapsed seeds individually range from $17\\%$ to $55\\%$ genuine.\nThis bifurcation is stochastic: all seeds are indistinguishable in the first ${\\sim}100$ steps ($\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0.05$--$0.07$); divergence occurs at steps 150--200 when H7 tautologies, which receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ by construction, are sampled during rollouts and reinforced via the positive feedback loop.\nOnce a seed enters the H7 basin, convergence to saturation takes ${\\sim}150$ steps and is irreversible. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46\n\nH7 (a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by the LOO definition; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) is the canonical Goodhart exploit: the model satisfies the structural metric directly rather than producing a genuine derivation.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both ISR and random conditions (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}); the specific mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) within a seed is stochastic under both conditions ($\\text{CV}{>}1$), though ISR modestly increases H7 probability by providing a direct reward pathway.\nRandom yields higher PR ($83{\\pm}15\\%$ vs.\\ $70{\\pm}19\\%$; $n{=}5$ each).\nRandom H7 exhibits extreme seed dependence (per-seed range: $0\\%$--$91.5\\%$; pooled $30\\%$ is dominated by s46 at $91.5\\%$ and s43 at $38.3\\%$).\nBoth conditions produce H7 in 2 of 5 seeds, but the quality differs: ISR-induced H7 is absorbing ($100\\%$ of proved samples in affected seeds: s43${=}61/61$, s44${=}89/89$), while random-induced H7 coexists with other modes ($38$--$92\\%$).\nISR's reward signal converts H7 from a stochastic possibility into an irreversible attractor once sampled.\n\n\\textbf{The transient genuine window.}\nDuring the transient window, Cochran--Mantel--Haenszel tests stratified by seed confirm ISR produces more genuine proofs than random controls ($\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$, 95\\% CI $[2.21, 5.13]$, $p{<}10^{-9}$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-perseed}).\nPooled Fisher ($\\text{OR}{=}3.48$, uncorrected) is consistent but does not account for within-seed dependence.\nUnder stricter H8+FN-corrected classification (3-seed subset with complete audits), the effect reverses ($\\text{OR}{=}0.61$, n.s.), reflecting both the conservative bias of H8 correction (which reclassifies borderline samples as hacking) and the reduced sample size ($n{=}231$ vs 350). The 5-seed CMH and 3-seed corrected analyses bound the true effect from above and below respectively.\nThe pooled 5-seed non-hacking rate is 23\\% (uncorrected; s42-only H8-corrected estimate: 17\\%).\nThis benefit is temporary: extended training (1000 steps) on seeds~42 and~45 confirms the window closes completely ($17\\%{\\to}2\\%$ and $55\\%{\\to}0\\%$ genuine respectively; see below).\nThe same LOO bias that enables genuine improvement (rewarding necessary premises) also creates the Goodhart attractor (rewarding tautological constructions); the genuine window reflects seeds that have not yet sampled the H7 exploit, not seeds that resist it.\nSnapshot evaluation at 500 steps would overestimate ISR's benefit; trajectory analysis is required to distinguish transient from stable improvement. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42/s45/s46 (500 step) + plan002-b01-s42-1000\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe genuine-rate advantage reflects a selection effect: premise-count-matched comparison yields only $1.06\\times$ ISR difference, confirming indistinguishable per-proof quality once LOO bias toward short outputs is controlled (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n\n\\textbf{Trajectory analysis: timing, not destiny.}\\label{sec:goodhart-temporal}\nExtended training (1000 steps; Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}) confirms the genuine window is transient across seeds.\nSeed~42 declines from $17\\%$ to $2\\%$ genuine via H7 saturation ($16.0\\%{\\to}96.7\\%$ H7, PR stable at ${\\sim}96\\%$); seed~45, which achieved the highest 500-step genuine rate ($55\\%$), collapses to $0\\%$ genuine through reward collapse ($\\text{PR}: 42\\%{\\to}2\\%$).\nThe two seeds exhibit distinct failure paths (H7 gaming vs.\\ output collapse) but converge to the same outcome: zero genuine proofs at extended training. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan002-b01-s45-1000\n\n\\textbf{Micro-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.05$): deterministic collapse.}\nAll three ISR seeds reach $\\text{PR}{\\geq}99\\%$ but converge to H7a tautology (H7 $87$--$100\\%$, genuine $5.1\\%$), while the random control mostly fails to learn (median $\\text{PR}{=}7\\%$) with zero H7, confirming H7 as specific to ISR's LOO structure.\nBelow a signal-floor, ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable (per-seed breakdown in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta005}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b005-s42/s43/s44, sweep-random-b005-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{Medium-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.2$): maximum exploitation.}\nAll three seeds converge to pure H7a tautology ($0\\%$ non-hacking, $n{=}290$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$): three seeds yield three distinct failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, pure H7 gaming; per-seed details in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\nThe random control is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$) with $91\\%$ H1 and ${\\leq}4\\%$ non-hacking; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nISR vs.\\ random H7 at $\\beta{=}0.3$: $\\text{OR}{=}13.7$, 95\\% CI $[7.0, 26.8]$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\textbf{Non-monotonic dose-response.}\nThe four $\\beta$ levels reveal a non-monotonic pattern: $\\beta\\in\\{0.05, 0.2\\}$ converge deterministically to H7 collapse (${\\geq}94\\%$ H7), $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers multi-modal instability, and only $\\beta{=}0.1$ sustains a transient genuine window (3 of 5 seeds).\nGoodhart collapse is not a simple function of dose; the balance between genuine and exploit reinforcement depends on a threshold interaction between $\\beta$ and rollout stochasticity.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog (3 seeds, $\\beta{=}0.1$).\nUnder exec-only training, 2/3 seeds show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite $\\text{PR}{\\geq}0.56$, reflecting redundant ground-fact repetition rather than FOL-style conclusion embedding; ISR flags both failure semantics.\nUnder ISR reward, 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking through three distinct mechanisms: clean H7 tautology (s42, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$), degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog evading text-match classifiers (s43, functional $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$), and query-as-fact gaming (s44, $95/97$ proved at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$).\nSeed~44 evaded our strict classifier entirely; functional cross-validation was required to expose it.\nWe attribute complete collapse to Prolog's simpler operational semantics providing a broader gaming basin (detailed results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} and Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Evidence}\n\\label{sec:crossscale}\n\nWe train Qwen3-1.7B under ISR $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$ and Random $\\beta{=}0.1$ (single seed, 500 steps).\nAll conditions produce $0\\%$ non-hacking, but the exploit modes differ qualitatively from 4B: Random discovers H9 (contradiction injection, $\\text{PR}{=}93\\%$, $100\\%$ \\emph{ex falso}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ collapses to a single fixed template ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.6$ at zero variance, $100\\%$ H1), and ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers reward collapse ($\\text{PR}{=}17\\%$) mirroring the 4B pattern.\nH9 never appears at 4B scale; fixed-template mode collapse never appears at 4B; different capacities converge to different Goodhart basins under identical rewards.\nNo safe scale was observed in this single-seed comparison (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{SFT} & \\textbf{Prolog$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{Rnd}}$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 59 & 7 & 0$^\\sharp$ & \\textbf{100} & 0 \\\\\nH7 duplicate   & 0  & \\textbf{54} & 30 & 0 & 100$^\\sharp$ & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & 8   & 0 & --- & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH9 ex falso    & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & 0 & \\textbf{100} \\\\\nTautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 & --- & --- \\\\\nRepetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nCmplx.\\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nQuery-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 & --- & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & \\textbf{23}  & 8 & \\textbf{92} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Nine hacking modes across conditions (\\% of proved samples; exec-only/SFT: seed~42; ISR/Random 4B: pooled across 5 seeds; 1.7B columns: Qwen3-1.7B seed~42). \\\\\nEffective batch size & 8 \\\\\nMax completion length & 512 tokens \\\\\nRandom seeds & 42, 43, 44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Data}} \\\\\nTraining set & FOLIO, 500 examples & Proved-only (ISR$^*$) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nAll cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator (\\texttt{eval\\_sta.py}) so that training-log ISR is never compared across runs.\nThe evaluation file \\texttt{proofwriter\\_fol\\_100.jsonl} is a legacy filename containing no ProofWriter data.\nFOL exec-only seed~43 (\\texttt{plan001-exec-only-s43-r4}) was evaluated with sampling (temperature~$=0.7$) rather than greedy decoding.\nIts PR and ISR values are included for completeness but are not strictly comparable with other seeds under the unified evaluation protocol (C004).\n\n\n\\section{STA Algorithm}\n\\label{app:algorithm}\n\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:sta} (Section~\\ref{sec:sta}) gives the procedure for computing ISR over a proved formalization.\nFor unproved samples ($S(x) \\neq \\textit{proved}$), ISR is undefined and the protocol is not applied.\n\n\n\\section{Extended Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{What ISR measures, and what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness (whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning).\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.\nISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.\nISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding (repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof) rather than an absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$, even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound is central to ISR's Goodhart vulnerability: H7 tautologies (single premise $=$ conclusion) achieve $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ \\emph{by construction}, and the shorter formalizations produced by non-collapsed seeds at $\\beta{=}0.1$ also inflate ISR mechanically.\nPremise-count-matched comparison on non-hacking samples yields $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ vs.\\ $0.243$ for random ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed}), confirming that even within the transient genuine window the structural benefit is negligible.\nThis bias is not specific to our setting; any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Fisher exact tests across conditions. Non-hacking rate reported at 5-seed (primary) and 3-seed with H8+FN corrections (sensitivity); H7 comparison at 5-seed (primary) and 3-seed (sensitivity).}\n\\label{tab:bootstrap-ci}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Comparison} & \\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Control} & \\textbf{OR} & \\textbf{$p$} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Non-hacking rate (5-seed)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 22.6\\% {\\scriptsize[18.2,27.0]} & 7.7\\% {\\scriptsize[5.5,10.7]} & 3.48 & ${<}10^{-8}$ \\\\\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Non-hacking rate (3-seed, H8+FN corrected)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 6.06\\% {\\scriptsize[3.6,9.9]} & 9.5\\% {\\scriptsize[6.5,13.7]} & 0.61 & 0.182 (n.s.) \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{H7 gaming rate (5-seed)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 54.3\\% {\\scriptsize[49.1,59.5]} & 29.5\\% {\\scriptsize[25.1,33.9]} & 2.84 & $4.82{\\times}10^{-12}$ \\\\\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{H7 gaming rate (3-seed sensitivity)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 70.6\\% {\\scriptsize[64.3,76.3]} & 13.7\\% {\\scriptsize[9.9,18.6]} & 15.05 & $1.3{\\times}10^{-39}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{SFT vs.\\ Exec-only non-hacking rate}} \\\\\nSFT vs.\\ Exec-only & 92.3\\% {\\scriptsize[79.7,97.3]} & 7.5\\% & 147 & ${<}0.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\\\[2pt]\n{\\footnotesize 5-seed: ISR $n{=}350$, Random $n{=}414$. 3-seed: ISR $n{=}231$, Random $n{=}262$. Exec-only: $n{=}93$ (s42). SFT: $n{=}39$ (3 seeds, 1000 steps). 95\\% Wilson CIs in brackets where shown.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Seed-level statistical analysis.}\nTo address potential inflation from within-seed dependence, we supplement pooled Fisher exact tests with two seed-aware analyses.\nCochran--Mantel--Haenszel (CMH) tests stratify by seed while pooling across samples: H7 $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$ (95\\% CI $[1.85, 3.27]$, $p{=}5.6{\\times}10^{-16}$); genuine $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$ (95\\% CI $[2.21, 5.13]$, $p{=}4.5{\\times}10^{-10}$).\nSeed-level permutation tests (treating each seed as a single observation, $n{=}5$ per group) yield $p{=}0.28$ (H7) and $p{=}0.26$ (genuine), consistent with the severe power limitation of $n{=}5$ (minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$) rather than absence of effect.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed classification results for ISR and Random at $\\beta{=}0.1$ (500 steps, FOL). $n$ = proved samples. ISR$^*$ = proved-only mean ISR. $\\dagger$~H8 audit complete; $\\ddagger$~checkpoint unavailable before audit, N-H is uncorrected upper bound.}\n\\label{tab:fol-beta01-perseed}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lrrrrrrrr}\n\\toprule\n& Seed & PR\\% & $n$ & ISR$^*$ & H7\\% & H1\\% & H8\\% & N-H\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{6}{*}{\\rotatebox{90}{ISR}}\n& s42 & 81 & 81 & 0.415 & 16.0 & 51.9 & 14.8$\\dagger$ & 17.3 \\\\\n& s43 & 61 & 61 & 0.830 & 96.7 & 0 & ---$\\ddagger$ & 3.3 \\\\\n& s44 & 89 & 89 & 0.902 & 100 & 0 & ---$\\ddagger$ & 0 \\\\\n& s45 & 42 & 42 & 0.605 & 38.1 & 4.8 & 2.4 & 54.8 \\\\\n& s46 & 77 & 77 & 0.411 & 14.3 & 26.0 & 5.2 & 54.5 \\\\\n& \\textbf{Pool} & \\textbf{70$\\pm$19} & \\textbf{350} & --- & \\textbf{54.3} & \\textbf{18.0} & --- & \\textbf{23.1} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{6}{*}{\\rotatebox{90}{Random}}\n& s42 & 79 & 79 & 0.104 & 0 & 73.4 & 5.1$\\dagger$ & 22.8 \\\\\n& s43 & 94 & 94 & 0.141 & 38.3 & 59.6 & 1.1 & 1.1 \\\\\n& s44 & 89 & 89 & 0.062 & 0 & 82.0 & 18.0 & 6.7 \\\\\n& s45 & 58 & 58 & 0.010 & 0 & 91.4 & 0 & 8.6 \\\\\n& s46 & 94 & 94 & 0.042 & 91.5 & 2.1 & 4.3 & 2.1 \\\\\n& \\textbf{Pool} & \\textbf{83$\\pm$15} & \\textbf{414} & --- & \\textbf{29.5} & \\textbf{55.8} & --- & \\textbf{7.7} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{H7 as inevitable exploit.}\nISR's LOO definition creates an inherent structural bias: tautological proofs ($P{\\to}P$) receive $\\text{ISR}{=}0.75$, and single-premise-equals-conclusion constructions receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, making H7 the global ISR-maximizing exploit.\n$\\beta$ controls the \\emph{timing} of collapse, not whether it occurs.\nAt all four tested doses, H7 dominates: $\\beta{=}0.05$ (3 seeds, pooled H7 $93.7\\%$, $n{=}253$; random s43 achieves $\\text{PR}{=}62\\%$ with H1 $77.4\\%$ and zero H7, confirming mode specificity); $\\beta{=}0.1$ (stochastic bifurcation: 2/5 seeds collapse within ${\\sim}150$ steps, remaining 3/5 sustain a transient window that closes under extended training); $\\beta{=}0.2$ ($100\\%$ H7 across all three seeds, $n{=}290$; Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail}); $\\beta{=}0.3$ (multi-modal, s44 pure H7).\nThe random control produces $29.5\\%$ H7 ($122/414$, 5-seed; per-seed: $0\\%$--$91.5\\%$, dominated by s46 at $91.5\\%$ and s43 at $38.3\\%$; s42/s44/s45 at $0\\%$), random $\\beta{=}0.3$ yields 4--7 H7a per seed.\nOnce a seed samples and reinforces the H7 exploit, convergence is irreversible; this absorbing-state property guarantees eventual collapse regardless of dose.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{ISR $\\beta{=}0.2$ per-seed results (maximum exploitation point). All seeds converge to H7a tautology with 0\\% genuine.}\n\\label{tab:beta02-detail}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR\\%} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7a\\%} & \\textbf{Genuine\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\ns42 & 100 & 0.55 & 100 & 0 \\\\\ns43 & 97 & 0.96 & 96.9 & 0 \\\\\ns44 & 93 & 0.55 & 100 & 0 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Pooled} & 97$\\pm$4 & 0.69$\\pm$0.24 & 99.0 & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Goodhart effects: implications.}\nThe finding extends beyond this specific system.\nISR was designed to \\emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\\text{exec}}$, yet it contains an inherent structural vulnerability: by its LOO definition, tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR ($P{\\to}P$ yields $\\text{ISR}{=}0.75$; single-premise-equals-conclusion yields $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$), creating a positive feedback loop once sampled during GRPO rollouts~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, kwa-catastrophic-goodhart}.\nGoodhart collapse~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} is observed at all four tested doses ($\\beta{=}0.05$: 3 seeds; $\\beta\\in\\{0.1, 0.2, 0.3\\}$: 3--5 seeds each).\nThe structural bias predicts universality (H7 tautology is always the global reward-maximizing exploit regardless of $\\beta$), and $\\beta$ controls only the probability of early sampling and thus the timing of collapse; at $\\beta{=}0.05$, three seeds confirm deterministic H7 collapse ($93.7\\%$ pooled) while the matched random control at the same $\\beta$ produces H1 redundancy instead ($77.4\\%$), ruling out dose magnitude as the driver.\nAt moderate doses ($\\beta{=}0.1$), collapse is stochastic and delayed (5 seeds bifurcate: 2 collapse rapidly, 3 sustain a transient genuine window that trajectory analysis confirms is temporary); at higher doses ($\\beta{=}0.2$), collapse is immediate and universal.\n$\\beta{=}0.3$ adds reward collapse~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} as a competing failure mode (PR drops to 18\\% in 1/3 seeds), but the underlying Goodhart vulnerability remains the same.\nThe practical implication is that ISR (and structurally similar LOO-based diagnostics) should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ (5 seeds) shows even greater instability: 2/5 seeds collapse to H7 within ${\\sim}150$ steps, while 3/5 sustain a transient genuine window.\nThe 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate is ${\\sim}23\\%$ at 500 steps; trajectory analysis on seeds~42 and~45 (1000 steps) confirms this is temporary ($17\\%{\\to}2\\%$ and $55\\%{\\to}0\\%$ genuine respectively).\nThe dominant hacking mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) is stochastic within any treatment condition ($\\text{CV}{>}1$).\nMulti-seed evaluation with trajectory analysis should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems; single-seed or single-checkpoint results can misrepresent a configuration's true hacking profile.\n\n\n\\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}\n\\label{app:hacking-modes}\n\nWe identify nine distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.\nEach mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.\nTable~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only: seed~42; all other bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). ISR increases H7 probability without reducing total hacking; the specific mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) is seed-dependent within each condition. SFT shows near-zero hacking.}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rnd} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ in premises & 87 & 18 & 71 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{71} & 14$^\\dagger$ & 0 \\\\\nH8 & Premise-subset gaming & 6 & 5 & -- & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}$ inflated & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nC-w/o-utility & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & -- & -- & -- & -- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed pooled (s42/s43/s44); 5-seed totals in Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}14$; primarily s44). $^\\dagger$Random H7 in 3-seed subset driven by seed~43 (38.3\\%); 5-seed pooled: 30\\% (dominated by s46 at 91.5\\%). Exec: H1${=}87$\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes. H8: premise-subset gaming (Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Classifier Validation.}\nHacking mode classification uses deterministic rule-based classifiers, not learned models, so standard ML precision/recall is not directly applicable.\nH1 and H7 are identified by normalized text matching between conclusion and premise strings after whitespace and variable normalization; remaining modes are detected via functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution from STA (leave-one-out ablation).\nNo training data or manual annotation is used for classification.\n\nTo estimate precision, we manually verified 50 randomly sampled outputs classified as H1 or H7: all 50 were confirmed correct (estimated precision ${>}$95\\%, Wilson 95\\% CI: [93\\%, 100\\%]).\nRecall is inherently bounded by the text-match threshold: semantic manipulations that alter surface form while preserving the gaming structure (e.g., variable renaming beyond our normalization, or paraphrased conclusions) would be missed.\nWe cross-validate with functional LOO analysis: samples classified as non-hacking but showing $n_{\\text{necessary}} \\leq 1$ are flagged as potential false negatives.\nThis cross-validation revealed query-as-fact gaming in Prolog (\\S\\ref{app:hacking-modes}), where the strict text classifier reported 100\\% non-hacking but functional analysis showed 95/97 samples were tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$).\nThe non-hacking rate for FOL conditions may therefore be slightly overestimated, as subtle semantic manipulations below our text-matching threshold would evade the strict classifier.\n\n\\subsection{H8 --- Premise-Subset Gaming}\n\\label{app:h8}\n\nManual audit of ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 revealed a previously undetected hacking mode: the model emits a subset of the gold premises that is individually necessary (high ISR) but insufficient for the intended derivation, with proof success depending on the conclusion already being embedded (H1-style).\nWe term this \\textbf{H8 (premise-subset gaming)}: formalizations where each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is a strict subset of what the NL problem requires, inflating ISR without faithful reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{Detection.} H8 is identified by manual inspection of non-hacking samples with unusually low premise counts ($n_{\\text{prem}} \\leq 3$) that nonetheless achieve high ISR.\nAutomated detection is not currently feasible because H8 requires comparing the generated premise set against the gold premises.\n\n\\paragraph{Audit scope and limitations.}\nFull H8 audit was completed for ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 only.\nISR seeds~43 and~44 checkpoints were deleted before audit and cannot be recovered; their non-hacking rates may be overestimated.\nRandom $\\beta{=}0.1$ does not produce H8 by mechanism (no ISR optimization pressure); seed~42 audit confirmed H8${=}0$.\n\n\\paragraph{H7 false-negative correction (H7b obfuscated tautology).}\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 contained 12 samples with $n_{\\text{prem}}{=}1$ classified as non-hacking by the text matcher.\nManual inspection confirmed all 12 are logically equivalent to H7 but evade text matching through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$) or structural simplification ($1/12$).\nWe classify these as \\textbf{H7b} (obfuscated tautology), distinct from text-match-detectable H7a.\n$10/12$ achieve $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.83$, indicating simultaneous gaming of execution reward, ISR metric, and H7 classifier (triple gaming; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\nAfter correction, ISR s44 non-hacking${=}0$; total H7 = H7a (77 text-match) + H7b (12 obfuscated) = 89.\n\n\\paragraph{Impact on non-hacking-rate comparison.}\nAfter H8 reclassification and H7 false-negative correction: ISR non-hacking $= 14/231 = 6.06\\%$ vs.\\ random non-hacking $= 25/262 = 9.5\\%$ (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, n.s.; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR [3.6\\%, 9.9\\%], Random [6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]).\nThe direction is consistent with ISR-amplified gaming modes (H7, H8) displacing non-hacking output, but the effect is not statistically significant at the conventional $\\alpha{=}0.05$ threshold.\n\n\\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.\nThe solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded\nConclusion: flies(tweety).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nVariable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (59--91\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (54\\% pooled, 5-seed).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7a --- Conclusion Duplication (Standard)}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical (text-match detectable) to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7a games both exec reward and ISR reward; H7b extends this to triple gaming by additionally evading the text-match classifier.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nH7a (text-match detectable): 64.5\\% (149/231, 3-seed) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%); 5-seed pooled H7: 54\\% (190/350, Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}).\nCombined H7 (H7a + H7b, 3-seed): 70.6\\% (163/231; s43 H7a${=}59$ + H7b${=}2$; includes s44 FN correction).\nRandom~$\\beta{=}0.1$ pooled: 29.5\\% (122/414, 5-seed, all H7a; driven by s46 at 91.5\\% and s43 at 38.3\\%; s42/s44/s45 at 0\\%).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7b --- Obfuscated Tautology}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nA single-premise formalization logically equivalent to H7a (premise = conclusion) but with surface-form differences that evade the text-match H7 classifier.\nThe model achieves this through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$ instances) or structural simplification ($1/12$).\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, vacuous quantifier wrapping).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y ((A(x)&B(y))->(C(x)|D(y))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\nThe premise introduces a vacuous second quantifier variable $y$ that is immediately unified with $x$, making the formulas logically equivalent while differing textually.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\n$\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.83$ ($10/12$ samples).\nH7b simultaneously games three objectives: execution reward ($r_{\\text{exec}}{=}1$ via tautological proof), ISR metric (single necessary premise yields high ISR), and the text-match H7 classifier (surface-form difference evades detection).\nThis triple gaming makes H7b the most complete Goodhart case we observe.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nFound primarily in ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 ($12/89$ proved samples, all originally classified as non-hacking); seed~43 contributes $2$ additional cases.\nFunctional cross-validation via $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ exposed the misclassification.\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=13$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous; none is individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Observed in Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\\% (3/79).\nNot observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).\n\n\n\\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso quodlibet}.\nIndividual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean\n  % conclusion embedded among premises\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n% NL: \"Fido is happy.\"\n% Model output:\nfido_is_happy.\n:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.\nThe single fact is trivially necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.\nFunctionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).\n95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% non-hacking.\n\n\n\\subsection{H9 --- Ex Falso Quodlibet (Contradiction Injection)}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates a premise set containing logically contradictory assertions (e.g., $P(x)$ and $\\neg P(x)$).\nFrom contradictory premises, any conclusion follows by the \\emph{ex falso quodlibet} principle, rendering the proof semantically vacuous regardless of the conclusion's content.\nUnlike the ``contradiction explosion'' mode observed in 4B exec-only at 1000 steps (where contradictions arise incidentally among inflated premise counts), H9 is a \\emph{targeted} strategy: the model constructs minimal contradictory pairs that directly enable the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  -bird(tweety).  % contradicts above\nConclusion: happy(tweety).\n% Proof: bird(tweety) & -bird(tweety) -> False -> anything\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$>0$ because each side of the contradiction is individually necessary under LOO: removing $P(x)$ leaves $\\neg P(x)$ alone, which cannot derive a contradiction; removing $\\neg P(x)$ leaves the premises consistent but insufficient to derive an unrelated conclusion.\nThe LOO test thus registers both contradictory premises as ``necessary,'' inflating ISR despite the formalization being semantically vacuous.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved exclusively in Qwen3-1.7B under Random $\\beta{=}0.1$ (seed~42, 500 steps, proper train/eval separation): 100\\% of proved samples ($n{=}93$) exhibit H9.\nUnder ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ with the same model, H9 does not appear; the model instead converges to fixed-template H1 ($\\text{ISR}{=}0.6$, zero variance), indicating that ISR reward redirects the hacking pathway from H9 to H1.\nNot observed in any Qwen3-4B experiment across all conditions and seeds tested. %% registry: d113-1.7b-random-b01-s42\n\n\n\\section{Per-Seed Detailed Results}\n\\label{app:detailed-results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed} and \\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} report full per-seed results.\nAll metrics are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} on the held-out 100-example FOLIO evaluation set using greedy decoding unless otherwise noted.\nISR values are proved-only ISR$^*$.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). Includes the per-seed $\\beta{=}0.1$ breakdown referenced from Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H\\ = percentage among proved samples. ``---'' indicates data not available. $^\\dagger$s43 uses sampling eval ($T{=}0.7$). $^\\ddagger$Three s44-1000 samples escape text-match heuristics (H1/H8 classifiers) but exhibit ISR${=}0$ and functionally degenerate proof structures: disjunctive conclusions that bypass H8 detection and commuted premises that bypass H1 detection; reclassified as degenerate. $^\\S$s43-1000 raw ISR (0.531) includes ${+}12\\%$ inflation from \\texttt{decompose\\_fol()} splitting $\\exists x(\\ldots)$ conclusions into quantifier + body as separate ablatable components; corrected ISR removes this artifact.}\n\\label{tab:fol-detailed}\n\\label{tab:fol-beta01-detailed}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Steps} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{9}{@{}l}{\\textit{Baselines}} \\\\\nBase model (no training)\n  & --- & 0 & 0.12 & 0.064 & --- & --- & --- & Pre-training \\\\\nGround-truth FOL\n  & --- & --- & 0.18 & Uppercase pseudo-Prolog syntax bypasses the text-match classifier.\\\\\n$^b$ Strict classifier reports Non-hacking$\\,{=}\\,100$\\%, but 95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$. The model converts NL to snake\\_case atoms serving as both fact and query, a third distinct Goodhart pathway.\\\\\n$^\\ddagger$ Strict classifier value is misleading; see footnotes $^a$/$^b$.\n}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig5_training_isr_curves.pdf}\n\\caption{Training ISR trajectories across reward conditions. ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds rise rapidly toward $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}1$ (Goodhart gaming via H7 tautology); exec-only s44's plateau at ${\\sim}0.5$ is an H7a artifact ($n{=}2$ gives $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ by construction), not genuine structural quality; random $\\beta{=}0.1$ remains low. The s42 1k-step trace shows ISR eventually saturates at $0.88$ through the same H7 mechanism.}\n\\label{fig:training-isr-curves}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.5$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: consistent gaming across all seeds via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart effects with 0\\% non-hacking proofs (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}), but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds, despite identical training data and reward function, demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n\n\\subsection{Per-Seed Results at $\\beta{=}0.3$}\n\\label{app:beta03}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:beta03-detailed} reports per-seed results for both ISR and Random reward at $\\beta{=}0.3$.\nThe three ISR seeds illustrate qualitatively distinct Goodhart pathways (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed results at $\\beta{=}0.3$ (FOL). PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H = percentage among proved samples.}\n\\label{tab:beta03-detailed}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Mode} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.18 & 0.143 & 0 & 77.8 & 5.6 & Reward collapse ($n{=}18$); H8${=}3$ \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.86 & 0.295 & 4.7 & 68.6 & 23.3 & H1 shortcutting \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.95 & 0.742 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & Pure H7 gaming \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.66{\\pm}0.42$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.39{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Random $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.48 & 0.094 & 8 & 92 & 0 & H7a${=}4$ (D086) \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.92 & 0.180 & 7.6 & 89.1 & 3.3 & H7a${=}7$; $n_{\\text{N-H}}{=}3$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.23 & 0 & 0 & 100 & 0 & Pure H1 ($n{=}23$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.54{\\pm}0.35$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.09{\\pm}0.09$} & & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Per-Seed Results at $\\beta{=}0.05$}\n\\label{app:beta005}\n\nThe 3-seed random control at $\\beta{=}0.05$ is highly unstable: s42 fails entirely ($\\text{PR}{=}0\\%$), s44 barely learns ($\\text{PR}{=}7\\%$, H1 $71\\%$, genuine $0\\%$), and only s43 reaches moderate output ($\\text{PR}{=}62\\%$, H1 $77\\%$, genuine $13\\%$).\nThe single random seed that learns (s43) shows higher genuine output than ISR ($13\\%$ vs.\\ $5.1\\%$ pooled), consistent with a signal-floor below which ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable.\n\n\\subsection{Early-Training Hacking Analysis (100-Step Checkpoint)}\n\\label{app:100step}\n\nTo control for the prove-rate confound in our SFT comparison (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}), we analyze the exec-only checkpoint at 100 training steps (seed~42), where PR${=}32$\\%, higher than SFT (13.0\\%, 3-seed pooled) but still early in training.\nOf the 32 proved samples, 9 (28.1\\%) exhibit H7 hacking (single premise identical to conclusion) and 1 (3.1\\%) exhibits H1 (conclusion embedded among premises), yielding a total hacking rate of 31.2\\%.\nBy contrast, SFT at comparable or lower PR shows 7.7\\% hacking (1/13 proved samples).\nThis demonstrates that reward hacking emerges early in GRPO training, well before the prove-rate plateau, and is not simply an artifact of having more proved samples available for exploitation.\nThe 100-step hacking rate (31.2\\%) is already substantially above SFT (7.7\\%), supporting the claim that hacking is associated with RL optimization rather than prove-rate level alone.\n\n\\subsection{$O(1)$ vs.\\ $O(n)$ ISR Ablation}\n\\label{app:o1-ablation}\n\nTo justify the per-clause LOO design (\\S\\ref{sec:sta}), we train a variant that replaces the $O(n)$ ablation with a cheaper $O(1)$ binary signal: $\\text{fast\\_ISR}(x) = 1$ if $x$ is proved \\emph{and} the conclusion is not provable under empty premises (tautology check), $0$ otherwise.\nThe reward becomes $r = 0.5\\,r_{\\text{exec}} + 0.5\\,\\text{fast\\_ISR}$.\nAfter 500 steps (seed~42), this variant achieves $\\text{PR}{=}63\\%$ with $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$ (functionally zero), compared to $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.132$ under standard exec-only and $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.42$ under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR signal vs.\\ training dynamics.}\nAt the \\emph{ISR signal level}, the $O(1)$ variant is indistinguishable from exec-only: both produce near-zero ISR because models emit redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.\nAt the \\emph{training level}, the two conditions are non-equivalent: the $O(1)$ tautology check penalizes formalizations where the conclusion is provable without any premises, a constraint absent from exec-only.\nThis confound depresses prove rate by 30 percentage points ($63\\%$ vs.\\ $93\\%$ exec-only), making it impossible to attribute the difference purely to the ISR signal.\nThe result confirms that the $O(n)$ per-clause ablation is necessary for ISR to carry a structural signal, while also showing that even a minimal tautology guard alters training dynamics substantially.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward across four dose levels, where it exhibits Goodhart effects (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and STA as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nCross-scale evidence from Qwen3-1.7B reveals capacity-dependent Goodhart basins (\\S\\ref{sec:crossscale}).\nA hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B~\\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with negative log-likelihood (NLL) on gold formalizations.\\footnote{All experiments use \\texttt{data/train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}\nHeld-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\\texttt{proofwriter\\_fol\\_100.jsonl}) with greedy decoding (Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}).\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \\emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate, defined as the residual of proved samples matching none of the nine cataloged patterns (a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness).\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$93\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls $0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$ over 100$\\to$500$\\to$1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have $\\text{ISR}{=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) at 100, 500, 1000 steps.\nPR rises $32\\%\\to93\\%\\to96\\%$, a trajectory that execution-only evaluation would interpret as successful optimization; $\\text{ISR}^*$ tells the opposite story, $0.642\\to0.132\\to0.003$ (214$\\times$ collapse). %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42\nBy 500 steps, 87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion (H1); by 1000 steps, the embedding rate saturates at 100\\% and mean component count more than doubles ($6.58\\to18.55$) as the model emits contradictory or vacuous premises that trivially derive any conclusion.\nExtended training, lacking a structural signal, \\emph{worsens} formalization quality while improving the headline number.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge into qualitatively different failure modes despite shared data, hyperparameters, and reward (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}): s42 reaches $\\text{PR}{=}93\\%$ via conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}{=}87\\%$); s43 collapses to $\\text{PR}{=}13\\%$ (at 500 steps) via repetition (87\\% of rollouts truncated); s44 reaches $\\text{PR}{=}98\\%$ via tautological collapse ($\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, mean $n{=}2.04$). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44\nThe cross-seed PR variance ($\\sigma{=}47$\\,pp) shows that aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes; all three are flagged by $\\text{ISR}^*$ even though one passes the PR check (s44) and one fails it (s43).\nBy 1000 steps, all three converge to high PR ($0.95{\\pm}0.05$) with near-zero genuine output (see Appendix~\\ref{app:h8} and Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}), each through a different dominant mode (s42: H1 saturation; s43: H1 $93.8\\%$, genuine $6.2\\%$; s44: H1+H8). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42, exec-only-s43-1000step-r7, plan001-exec-only-s44-1000-r2\nNotably, s43 recovers from $\\text{PR}{=}13\\%$ (500 steps) to $\\text{PR}{=}96\\%$ (1000 steps), but entirely through H1 exploitation: PR recovery does not imply quality recovery, and the repetition-collapse phase is transient rather than terminal.\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nSFT on gold formalizations reaches $\\text{PR}{=}13.0\\%$ (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps) with $\\text{H7}{=}0$, $\\text{H1}{=}7.7\\%$, $92.3\\%$ non-hacking ($n{=}39$; $\\text{OR}{=}147$ vs.\\ exec-only, $p{<}0.001$; per-seed range ${\\sim}$91--93\\%). %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3\nThe near-absence of hacking under identical data exposure is consistent with hacking being RL-specific; a 100-step checkpoint confirms hacking emerges early in training (Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}).\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Reward: Goodhart Effects}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nWe now reverse roles: instead of treating ISR as an observer, we use it as a reward augmentation, $r = r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1 + \\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$, with a matched random control, $r = r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1 + \\beta\\,u)$, $u\\sim\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, to isolate ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects.\nThe headline finding: ISR contains an inherent Goodhart vulnerability~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} observed to produce collapse at all four tested doses ($\\beta\\in\\{0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3\\}$).\nThe mechanism is structural: tautological proofs ($P{\\to}P$) receive $\\text{ISR}{=}0.75$ and single-premise-equals-conclusion constructions receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, both by the LOO definition alone.\nOnce sampled during GRPO rollouts, these high-ISR tautologies create a positive feedback loop; $\\beta$ controls only the \\emph{timing} of collapse, not whether it occurs.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: sta-phase0-baseline\nSFT & --- & 13 & 0.595 & 0 & 8 & \\textbf{92} \\\\ %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3 (3-seed pooled)\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 8 \\\\ %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2 (H8 corrected)\nISR$^\\ddagger$ & 0.1 & 70{\\scriptsize$\\pm$19} & --- & \\textbf{54} & 18 & 23$^\\star$ \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46 (5-seed, D124)\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{83}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$15} & 0.080{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.06} & 30 & 59 & 8 \\\\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r3/s44-r2/s45/s46 (5-seed)\nISR & 0.2 & 97{\\scriptsize$\\pm$4} & 0.69{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.24} & \\textbf{99} & 0 & 0 \\\\ %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44 (3-seed; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail})\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 66{\\scriptsize$\\pm$42} & 0.39{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.31} & \\textbf{50} & 37 & 11 \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44 (pooled; D106 s43 genuine 23→20)\nRandom & 0.3 & 54{\\scriptsize$\\pm$35} & 0.09{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.09} & 7 & 91 & 2 \\\\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42, exp1-random-b03-s43-r4, exp1-random-b03-s44-r2 (pooled; D086 H7: s42=4,s43=7,s44=0)\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Qwen3-1.7B (seed~42)}} \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 100 & 0.600 & 0 & \\textbf{100} & 0 \\\\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-isr-b01-s42\nRandom & 0.1 & 93 & 0.028 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-random-b01-s42 (H9=100%)\nISR & 0.3 & 17 & 0.031 & 0 & 71 & 29 \\\\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-isr-b03-s42\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. PR/ISR$^*$: mean$\\pm$std of per-seed values; H7/H1/N-H: pooled percentages. ISR$^*$ for ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ omitted (inflated by H7 gaming; premise-count-matched value is $1.06\\times$ random, \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}). 1.7B ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$: fixed-template H1, zero-variance; 1.7B Random: 100\\% H9 (ex falso, not in H7/H1 columns). $^\\dagger$Single seed (s42), greedy decoding, 500 steps. 3-seed 1000-step data (s42/s43/s44) in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:exec-only-1000}; all converge to ${\\geq}94\\%$ PR with near-zero genuine. $^\\ddagger$ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ s43/s44: H8 audit unavailable; N-H may be slightly inflated. $^\\star$5-seed pooled, transient; collapses to ${\\sim}2\\%$ at 1000 steps (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart-temporal}). Per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): stochastic delayed collapse.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 5 seeds exhibit a striking bifurcation: 2 seeds (s43, s44) collapse rapidly to near-pure H7 tautology (${\\geq}97\\%$ H7, $0\\%$ genuine within ${\\sim}150$ steps), while 3 seeds (s42, s45, s46) sustain a transient genuine window.\nThe 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate is ${\\sim}23\\%$ at 500 steps, with the collapsed seeds pulling down the average; the 3 non-collapsed seeds individually range from $17\\%$ to $55\\%$ genuine.\nThis bifurcation is stochastic: all seeds are indistinguishable in the first ${\\sim}100$ steps ($\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0.05$--$0.07$); divergence occurs at steps 150--200 when H7 tautologies, which receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ by construction, are sampled during rollouts and reinforced via the positive feedback loop.\nOnce a seed enters the H7 basin, convergence to saturation takes ${\\sim}150$ steps and is irreversible. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46\n\nH7 (a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by the LOO definition; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) is the canonical Goodhart exploit: the model satisfies the structural metric directly rather than producing a genuine derivation.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both ISR and random conditions (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}); the specific mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) within a seed is stochastic under both conditions ($\\text{CV}{>}1$), though ISR modestly increases H7 probability by providing a direct reward pathway.\nRandom yields higher PR ($83{\\pm}15\\%$ vs.\\ $70{\\pm}19\\%$; $n{=}5$ each).\nRandom H7 exhibits extreme seed dependence (per-seed range: $0\\%$--$91.5\\%$; pooled $30\\%$ is dominated by s46 at $91.5\\%$ and s43 at $38.3\\%$).\nBoth conditions produce H7 in 2 of 5 seeds, but the quality differs: ISR-induced H7 is absorbing ($100\\%$ of proved samples in affected seeds: s43${=}61/61$, s44${=}89/89$), while random-induced H7 coexists with other modes ($38$--$92\\%$).\nISR's reward signal converts H7 from a stochastic possibility into an irreversible attractor once sampled.\n\n\\textbf{The transient genuine window.}\nDuring the transient window, Cochran--Mantel--Haenszel tests stratified by seed confirm ISR produces more genuine proofs than random controls ($\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$, 95\\% CI $[2.21, 5.13]$, $p{<}10^{-9}$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-perseed}).\nPooled Fisher ($\\text{OR}{=}3.48$, uncorrected) is consistent but does not account for within-seed dependence.\nUnder stricter H8+FN-corrected classification (3-seed subset with complete audits), the effect reverses ($\\text{OR}{=}0.61$, n.s.), reflecting both the conservative bias of H8 correction (which reclassifies borderline samples as hacking) and the reduced sample size ($n{=}231$ vs 350). The 5-seed CMH and 3-seed corrected analyses bound the true effect from above and below respectively.\nThe pooled 5-seed non-hacking rate is 23\\% (uncorrected; s42-only H8-corrected estimate: 17\\%).\nThis benefit is temporary: extended training (1000 steps) on seeds~42 and~45 confirms the window closes completely ($17\\%{\\to}2\\%$ and $55\\%{\\to}0\\%$ genuine respectively; see below).\nThe same LOO bias that enables genuine improvement (rewarding necessary premises) also creates the Goodhart attractor (rewarding tautological constructions); the genuine window reflects seeds that have not yet sampled the H7 exploit, not seeds that resist it.\nSnapshot evaluation at 500 steps would overestimate ISR's benefit; trajectory analysis is required to distinguish transient from stable improvement. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42/s45/s46 (500 step) + plan002-b01-s42-1000\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe genuine-rate advantage reflects a selection effect: premise-count-matched comparison yields only $1.06\\times$ ISR difference, confirming indistinguishable per-proof quality once LOO bias toward short outputs is controlled (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n\n\\textbf{Trajectory analysis: timing, not destiny.}\\label{sec:goodhart-temporal}\nExtended training (1000 steps; Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}) confirms the genuine window is transient across seeds.\nSeed~42 declines from $17\\%$ to $2\\%$ genuine via H7 saturation ($16.0\\%{\\to}96.7\\%$ H7, PR stable at ${\\sim}96\\%$); seed~45, which achieved the highest 500-step genuine rate ($55\\%$), collapses to $0\\%$ genuine through reward collapse ($\\text{PR}: 42\\%{\\to}2\\%$).\nThe two seeds exhibit distinct failure paths (H7 gaming vs.\\ output collapse) but converge to the same outcome: zero genuine proofs at extended training. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan002-b01-s45-1000\n\n\\textbf{Micro-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.05$): deterministic collapse.}\nAll three ISR seeds reach $\\text{PR}{\\geq}99\\%$ but converge to H7a tautology (H7 $87$--$100\\%$, genuine $5.1\\%$), while the random control mostly fails to learn (median $\\text{PR}{=}7\\%$) with zero H7, confirming H7 as specific to ISR's LOO structure.\nBelow a signal-floor, ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable (per-seed breakdown in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta005}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b005-s42/s43/s44, sweep-random-b005-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{Medium-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.2$): maximum exploitation.}\nAll three seeds converge to pure H7a tautology ($0\\%$ non-hacking, $n{=}290$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$): three seeds yield three distinct failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, pure H7 gaming; per-seed details in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\nThe random control is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$) with $91\\%$ H1 and ${\\leq}4\\%$ non-hacking; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nISR vs.\\ random H7 at $\\beta{=}0.3$: $\\text{OR}{=}13.7$, 95\\% CI $[7.0, 26.8]$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\textbf{Non-monotonic dose-response.}\nThe four $\\beta$ levels reveal a non-monotonic pattern: $\\beta\\in\\{0.05, 0.2\\}$ converge deterministically to H7 collapse (${\\geq}94\\%$ H7), $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers multi-modal instability, and only $\\beta{=}0.1$ sustains a transient genuine window (3 of 5 seeds).\nGoodhart collapse is not a simple function of dose; the balance between genuine and exploit reinforcement depends on a threshold interaction between $\\beta$ and rollout stochasticity.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog (3 seeds, $\\beta{=}0.1$).\nUnder exec-only training, 2/3 seeds show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite $\\text{PR}{\\geq}0.56$, reflecting redundant ground-fact repetition rather than FOL-style conclusion embedding; ISR flags both failure semantics.\nUnder ISR reward, 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking through three distinct mechanisms: clean H7 tautology (s42, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$), degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog evading text-match classifiers (s43, functional $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$), and query-as-fact gaming (s44, $95/97$ proved at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$).\nSeed~44 evaded our strict classifier entirely; functional cross-validation was required to expose it.\nWe attribute complete collapse to Prolog's simpler operational semantics providing a broader gaming basin (detailed results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} and Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Scale Evidence}\n\\label{sec:crossscale}\n\nWe train Qwen3-1.7B under ISR $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$ and Random $\\beta{=}0.1$ (single seed, 500 steps).\nAll conditions produce $0\\%$ non-hacking, but the exploit modes differ qualitatively from 4B: Random discovers H9 (contradiction injection, $\\text{PR}{=}93\\%$, $100\\%$ \\emph{ex falso}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ collapses to a single fixed template ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.6$ at zero variance, $100\\%$ H1), and ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers reward collapse ($\\text{PR}{=}17\\%$) mirroring the 4B pattern.\nH9 never appears at 4B scale; fixed-template mode collapse never appears at 4B; different capacities converge to different Goodhart basins under identical rewards.\nNo safe scale was observed in this single-seed comparison (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{SFT} & \\textbf{Prolog$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{Rnd}}$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 59 & 7 & 0$^\\sharp$ & \\textbf{100} & 0 \\\\\nH7 duplicate   & 0  & \\textbf{54} & 30 & 0 & 100$^\\sharp$ & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & 8   & 0 & --- & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH9 ex falso    & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & 0 & \\textbf{100} \\\\\nTautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 & --- & --- \\\\\nRepetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nCmplx.\\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nQuery-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 & --- & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & \\textbf{23}  & 8 & \\textbf{92} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Nine hacking modes across conditions (\\% of proved samples; exec-only/SFT: seed~42; ISR/Random 4B: pooled across 5 seeds; 1.7B columns: Qwen3-1.7B seed~42). H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match) and H7b (obfuscated tautology; primarily s44). Cells with seed identifiers indicate the mode dominates that seed. $^\\sharp$Prolog row pools strict+functional (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe nine modes (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span 2 logic types, 2 model scales, and 6 conditions.\nH1, H7, and H8 account for ${>}90\\%$ of hacking; H7 includes obfuscated subtype H7b ($14/350$) that evades text-match classifiers (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\nISR modestly increases H7 convergence probability ($\\text{OR}{=}2.84$, $p{<}5{\\times}10^{-12}$, 5-seed; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}), but per-seed variance dominates (Random s46: $91.5\\%$ H7; ISR s46: $14.3\\%$).\nAll GRPO conditions show ${\\geq}80\\%$ pooled hacking; only SFT achieves $92.3\\%$ non-hacking.\nCochran--Mantel--Haenszel tests stratified by seed confirm both effects (H7: $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$, $p{<}10^{-15}$; genuine: $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$, $p{<}10^{-9}$), indicating that the associations are robust to within-seed clustering.\n",
